# Parity status, item by item

Audit of 2026-10-20 (v0.2.6, d1261f2) against CLIProxyAPI v8.0.35 (`ae3ee3be`). Milestones audited: M1, M2, M3, M4, M5, M6 (every item in [checklist.md](checklist.md)). [docs/PARITY.md](../PARITY.md) explains the milestones and sums them up.

Statuses:

- **covered**: implemented, and Rust tests or Go-generated fixtures exercise it. A note starting "Deliberate difference" marks an owner-approved divergence from Go; it counts as covered because there is no gap to close.
- **partial**: implemented in part, or implemented without tests that pin Go's behaviour. For Go test suites: the behaviour exists and is exercised, but not every Go case is ported.
- **missing**: not implemented.

Area names the part of the code base that would close a partial or missing item.

Method: `docs/parity-audit/audit.py` regenerates this file. Routes come from `probe.py`, which starts the binary and requests every listed method and path without upstream credentials. Client routes use a wrong client key; management routes use a private valid management key so they reach the router (routed pairs answer from the auth guard or handler, unrouted ones 404/405). A route counts as covered when a test requests it. Go test suites are matched case by case against Go test names cited in Rust code and in Go-generated fixtures (fixture names carry `TestName:line`). Every other item, and every suite the matcher cannot see, was judged by reading the Rust code and tests; those judgments live in `docs/parity-audit/manual.tsv` with their evidence. "Not ported by name" means the area is implemented and tested through other cases (usually Go-generated end-to-end scenarios), but the Go suite's own cases are not reproduced one by one. A "Suite changed" note preserves previous-reference evidence and case counts; it does not prove the current v8.0.35 assertions.

| M3-0243 | internal/runtime/executor/codex_executor_auth_test.go | covered | crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_oauth_tests.rs refresh_patch_matches_go_executor_refresh; crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_tests.rs siwc_requests_match_go (crates/cpa-exec/tests/fixtures/codex_siwc_go.json), siwc_partition_refreshes_like_go; crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_siwc_tests.rs refresh_due_matches_go_expiry_parsing (crates/cpa-exec/tests/fixtures/codex_siwc_expiry_go.json); crates/cpa-server/src/runtime.rs a_codex_refresh_waits_for_the_file_lock_and_adopts_what_another_process_rotated, a_codex_refresh_spends_the_tokens_on_disk_not_the_stale_snapshot, a_codex_refresh_of_an_adopted_partition_lands_after_an_edit_to_the_snapshot, a_codex_refresh_lands_over_the_snapshot_tokens_restored_while_it_ran, a_codex_refresh_is_discarded_when_the_credential_was_replaced_meanwhile, a_codex_refresh_is_discarded_when_the_credential_version_moved_meanwhile, a_codex_refresh_is_admitted_over_tokens_put_back_meanwhile, a_discarded_adopt_only_codex_refresh_changes_nothing, a_codex_refresh_publishes_its_tokens_when_the_save_fails, shutdown_stops_codex_lock_waits_and_drains_started_grants, a_codex_refresh_writes_its_tokens_when_the_id_was_registered_again_meanwhile, a_codex_refresh_routes_through_the_proxy_of_the_file_it_adopted, a_codex_refresh_fails_when_its_file_is_emptied_while_the_grant_runs, a_cancelled_codex_refresh_writes_its_tokens_before_another_process_reads_the_file, a_codex_refresh_keeps_a_disabled_flag_it_finds_in_the_file, a_rejected_codex_token_refreshes_when_the_file_rotated_only_its_refresh_token, a_codex_refresh_fails_when_its_file_is_removed_while_it_waits_for_the_lock |  | SIWC execute, stream, compact, WebSocket and image requests, the missing-token failure and the selective and dual refresh are ported (Go-recorded request goldens). The file-lock refresh (L1150) is ported as one transaction under the credential lock, owned apart from the caller: a refresh waits for the lock, adopts a partition another process rotated on disk without refreshing it again, spends the file's tokens over a stale snapshot (A→B→C), forces a refresh after a 401 only while the adopted partition still holds the rejected access token, after a grant writes the refreshed partitions onto the file as read again under the lock (Go matchesBaselineOrAfter over the snapshot, the locked file and the result; other fields, such as disabled, as the file holds them; values as Go's float64 decode), and only then admits the result to the store as Go updateInternal does: a re-registered ID is refused and a credential whose version moved or whose tokens changed, past every partition (CanApplyTo), discards the refresh without side effects; an admitted one is written again over the file with legacy keys renamed (PersistCodexAuthFile) and published with its proxy; an adopt-only refresh reaches the file only when admitted. A failed save is logged and the patch over memory published, a removed file written again from memory, as Go's manager does. At shutdown, a refresh still waiting for the lock gives up and one holding it finishes. Plugin-virtual Codex credentials refresh in memory (docs/DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md). A corrupt, emptied or vanished file fails. The Home-mode SIWC refresh (L1071) is not ported: Go's server never reaches it (Home mode loads no local credentials, and a dispatched Codex auth is neither prepared nor refreshed after a 401). |

The before-payload-finalizer case is not replayed one by one; Rust now runs the SIWC rules before the payload rules (868e137). |
| M3-0381 | internal/runtime/executor/codex_executor_sse_events_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_tests.rs split_sse_events_read_as_one_event (TestCodexExecuteStreamTreatsAnEventSplitAcrossDataLinesAsOneEvent, TestCodexExecuteNonStreamReadsAnEventSplitAcrossDataLines, TestCodexExecuteStreamAssemblesSplitEventsWithAndWithoutBootstrapBuffering, TestCodexExecuteStreamMinimalReproFromIssue6575) and split_sse_events_spend_the_bootstrap_budget (each delivered line spends the budget in order: split handshakes completed over 1 MiB, 47/48/49 comments, a handshake at exactly 1 MiB and one byte over, a 40-line split event plus 6 or 7 comments, outcomes recorded from Go v8.0.35; TestCodexExecuteStreamSplitHandshakeExceedingLineBudgetReleasesBootstrapWithoutFailover, TestCodexExecuteStreamEventExceedsBudgetWhileAssemblingReleasesStream, TestCodexExecuteStreamMixedFramingCommentInsideSplitEventCountsBudget, TestCodexExecuteStreamEventExceedsByteBudgetWhileAssemblingReleasesStream); codex_sse_tests.rs an_invalid_split_event_stays_invalid (TestCodexExecuteStreamKeepsAnInvalidSplitEventInvalid) | codex | The budget cases use Go's codexOverloadEvent (Go's own split-budget cases send server_overloaded, which never fails over) plus a within-budget control that fails over. Not replayed: the two clock-driven cases (Rust's bootstrap has no injectable clock) and the [DONE] log assertion. The byte budget counts each line's Codex-format output as its translated size. That equals Go's translator output for Codex clients and for Responses-client events the translator leaves unchanged; Go's Responses translator inserts a missing response.model, so such events count more bytes in Go (a 524,250-byte comment, then data: {"type":"response.in_progress"}, then the overload fails over in Rust and releases in Go). For Claude and Chat clients the count is approximate. Both differences matter only when the cumulative total straddles 1 MiB. Transport-level, not ported: the shared whole-event framer rejects an unfinished event over 16 MiB, below Go's 50 MiB line limit, and does not reject a complete frame over 50 MiB; releasing bootstrap while the upstream is mid-assembly, and processing a terminal line before its blank line, wait for the event delimiter. A Codex-specific incremental line reader would close these. |

| M4-0229 | Legacy/v8/mixed YAML precedence is presence-based, including false/zero/null/empty maps/li… | partial | crates/cpa-core/src/config.rs v8_wins_by_presence_even_when_null_or_empty, legacy_spellings_still_work, null_or_scalar_parents_are_rejected; document.rs tests; manage_go.json config_writes | manage | Gap (ponytail in config.rs expand_merges): a quoted << key in config.yaml merges at load, where yaml.v3 loads it as a plain key. Go clears only double-quoted key styles on a v8 save, so a double-quoted "<<" merges a mapping or fails on a scalar after the next save, a single-quoted '<<' stays a plain key, and an unknown section archived before the save never merges. A JSON body sent to /config.yaml is the same gap (Go 422, Rust 400). Other JSON management bodies match Go: << stays literal through the root-shape check and the update, then the completed candidate is expanded, so keys already present win (manage_go.rs config_writes merge_keys_in_json_bodies). |

| ID | Item | Status | Evidence | Area | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M4-0511 | server.github-token | partial | crates/cpa-server/tests/manage_go.rs config_writes (scenario server_github_token, recorded from Go v8.0.35); crates/cpa-server/tests/management.rs github_token_stays_out_of_the_v0_config_json | server | Accepted, migrated from the legacy root github-token and read and written through the v8 Management API as in Go; the v0 config JSON omits it. Nothing sends it yet: Go's release check and panel download uses are deliberate differences (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md), and the plugin store use (requests no store-auth rule covers) lands with M6-0261. |
| M4-0512 | routing.session-affinity-rebind-on | missing | New v8.0.35 configuration contract; internal/config/config_types.go:448 (SessionAffinityRebindOn). No verified Rust test for this path and its new semantics. | server | Conservative baseline: schema acceptance or a matching leaf name alone is not implementation proof. |
| M4-0513 | server.max-request-body-bytes | covered | crates/cpa-core/src/config.rs Config::max_request_body_bytes (v8 key wins over the legacy root key; nonpositive disables); crates/cpa-server/src/body_limit.rs (Content-Length refused unread, streamed bodies stopped past the limit, read from the current config per request; Read and FirstJson keep the bytes before the limit and bind the first value Go's encoding/json scanner completes in them); crates/cpa-server/src/management/multipart.rs body (ParseMultipartForm over the bytes before the limit: limit error or MIME parse error by where the limit falls); tests/body_limit_go.rs body_limit_answers_match_go replays tests/fixtures/body_limit_go.json, recorded from Go v8.0.35 through api.NewServer (status, Content-Type, body and bytes read for 190 cases: client routes, zstd on the JSON routes including images and videos, multipart and form bodies, request-log capture, plugin auth fallback, realtime and Codex live, Management v0 and v8 routes, exact boundary, negative and legacy keys, first JSON values complete, cut off or invalid before the limit on 15 binding handlers (Go's scanner: escape digits, the 10,000-level nesting limit, a completed value bound without the bytes after it), and multipart forms cut in file data, part headers or the final boundary, ending before the limit, with a preamble line past Go's 4096-byte reader buffer, or with a header line read in buffer-sized fragments (Go 1.26.4, the v8.0.35 release toolchain), on the credential upload, Vertex import, images edits and OpenAI videos; and, without a limit, gin's first-value binding with folded field names and duplicate keys on credential status, fields and plugin enable, unknown members skipped unconverted, fields values decoded as Go decodes them) a_published_limit_applies_to_the_next_request and a_reload_during_a_request_keeps_its_limit (the middleware's limit is stored on the request), a_stale_config_write_fails_before_its_body_is_read; body_limit.rs the_crossing_frame_keeps_its_permitted_prefix |  | Deliberate difference (docs/DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md): client routes also stop at the fixed 64 MiB limit, and Management handlers Go left at 400 that read their body whole (PUT config.yaml, PUT on the v0 key lists, v0 settings without a complete first value) answer axum's plain-text 400 at the limit. Deliberate difference (docs/DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md, Management JSON bodies): a matching string member with invalid UTF-8 or a lone surrogate is refused where Go binds it with U+FFFD, and fields refuses numbers past float64 and values nested past 127 levels. |
| M4-0514 | requests.tls-handshake-timeout-seconds | partial | crates/cpa-exec/src/tls.rs handshake_timeout_reads_the_root_key_and_its_legacy_spelling, silent_handshake_fails_only_with_the_bound; codex_tls.rs for_url | server | Read per request for the Claude Code and Chrome (chatgpt.com) transports; the failure is a transport fault (retry round, no cooldown). wreq has no handshake-only hook, so the bound also covers DNS, the TCP connect and a proxy CONNECT, which Go leaves unbounded (docs/CONFIGURATION.md). |

| M4-0377 | internal/watcher/dispatcher_snapshot_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/watching.rs keep_incomplete; src/watching.rs incomplete_auth_writes_keep_the_credential_until_the_file_is_valid_json, a_file_completed_after_the_scan_keeps_its_credential; tests/management.rs watcher tests; manage_go.json synth | manage | Suite changed since 6fecc6e. As in v8.0.35 (7484a521), a credential is kept while the scan's own read found its file empty or not valid JSON; the provider-reload buffering has no Rust counterpart because every reload rebuilds from disk. An incomplete read is recorded by the hash of what the scan read, as Go forgets its hash, so a file truncated before the scan and finished after it is reloaded (src/watching.rs the_watcher_records_what_its_reload_read_not_what_it_observed). Go's cases are not ported one by one. |

| M4-0384 | sdk/api/handlers/apply_patch_capability_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/dispatch.rs, errors.rs, respond.rs; tests/routes.rs | server | Not ported by name. |
| M4-0385 | sdk/api/handlers/handlers_error_response_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/dispatch.rs upstream_headers, errors.rs, respond.rs write_error_headers; tests/routes.rs passthrough_headers_follow_go; tests/media_go.rs media_routes_match_go replays tests/fixtures/passthrough_errors_go.json (recorded from Go v8.0.35 with passthrough headers on): compat_status_error, compat_stream_status_error (no Addon), compat_stream_empty (streamBootstrapError Addon); tests/ws_e2e.rs ws_request_fault_headers (statusErrWithHeaders Addon in the Responses WebSocket error event); errors.rs tests::openai_envelope_matches_go (TestBuildErrorResponseBody_CompactsPrettyPrintedJSON and four more inputs recorded from Go v8.0.35) | server | Suite changed since 6fecc6e; existing fixtures do not establish every v8.0.35 assertion. See the current Go declarations in checklist.md. Addon follows Go: only errors with Headers() (WebSocket error frames, stream bootstrap failures) hand headers to the client. authUnavailableError and modelCooldownError also implement Headers() (Retry-After); Rust writes that Retry-After whether or not passthrough is on, as Go's SafeResponseHeaders does. Framing headers in an Addon are dropped, where Go copies them (an upstream Content-Length can cut Go's error body). JSON validity of a 400 error body differs from Go's json.Valid: openai_body (serde_json) wraps "\uD800", 1e400 and nesting deeper than 128 levels in an OpenAI envelope as message text, where Go compacts and passes them through; the Responses WebSocket error_body (gjson valid) accepts those as Go does but has no 10,000-level depth limit, so it passes through nesting Go wraps. Duplicate keys behave the same. |

| M4-0562 | internal/watcher/auth_file_snapshot_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-core/src/config/credentials.rs scan_auth_dir records incomplete files during its own read; crates/cpa-server/src/watching.rs keep_incomplete; src/watching.rs incomplete_auth_writes_keep_the_credential_until_the_file_is_valid_json, a_file_completed_after_the_scan_keeps_its_credential, a_removed_and_recreated_file_keeps_its_credential | server | Added since 6fecc6e. Rust has no per-file snapshot: each reload re-reads the auth folder, so a credential is removed only when its file is gone or no longer holds it. An incomplete read is recorded by the hash of what the scan read, as Go invalidates its cached hash, so a write completed after the scan is reloaded (src/watching.rs the_watcher_records_what_its_reload_read_not_what_it_observed). Go's cases are not ported one by one. |
| M4-0563 | internal/watcher/clients_delete_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/watching.rs reload_config and keep_incomplete; src/watching.rs incomplete_auth_writes_keep_the_credential_until_the_file_is_valid_json, a_file_completed_after_the_scan_keeps_its_credential, an_unreadable_file_drops_its_credential, a_file_replaced_by_a_special_file_link_drops_its_credential, a_removed_and_recreated_file_keeps_its_credential | server | Added since 6fecc6e. A whole-file removal is decided from the folder at reload time, so a delete event that outlives a re-created file cannot remove it. Go's queued per-ID delete mechanism needs no counterpart: each reload reconciles against disk, the store consumers reread paths, Git guards watcher removals, and cpa-home has no auth-dir mirror. Only a file read successfully as empty or invalid JSON keeps its credential. Go's REMOVE path (service_auth.go shouldSkipFileAuthDelete) also keeps it when the stat fails with an error other than not-found, when the single-file snapshot fails (including a plugin parse error under StrictFileParsing), or when the snapshot has no valid IDs; here a stat or read failure, {}, or valid JSON that synthesis rejects all drop it, which matches Go's full scan but not its REMOVE path. The scan counts only entries that are or link to regular files, the watcher's rule, so a file replaced by a link to /dev/null drops its credential at once; Go would read it as empty and keep the credential until the REMOVE event. Go's cases are not ported one by one. |
| M4-0564 | internal/watcher/config_reload_ordering_test.go | covered | 3/3 cases cited in src/watching.rs the_watcher_records_what_its_reload_read_not_what_it_observed, an_unreadable_empty_config_converges, a_stale_cached_hash_does_not_reload_forever |  | Go 1631875b records the parsed config's SourceHash; watching.rs reload_config returns the hashes of the bytes it read (None for a failed read, as the observation records it) and record() keeps those as applied, so a write after the read (including a restore of bytes observed earlier) is reloaded when next observed. When the read differs from the observation the hash cache is dropped, so a stale cached hash cannot reload every tick. Go's concurrent-reload case has no Rust interleaving: one watcher task reloads, under the disk lock that saves hold. |

| M4-0574 | sdk/cliproxy/auth/conductor_issue6742_test.go | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/runtime.rs a_rejected_token_is_refreshed_in_the_background (a 401 on a Codex token nine days from expiry refreshes it at once; a 401 right after waits refreshFailureBackoff), which_401s_schedule_a_background_refresh (a forced-cooldown request-scoped 401 counts, a Claude 401 does not, a stale scan keeps a newer 401); crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_oauth_tests.rs refresh_due_uses_jwt_exp_before_metadata_and_a_96h_lead | server | Go feb63984. Codex only. Not ported (post-replay): the background refresh after a 401 for other providers, Claude's included (Go scheduleUnauthorizedRefresh); isCredentialBlocked for unauthorized and expired tokens; the credential-wide scheduler upsert after a 401. A Codex credential whose refresh keeps failing retries forever under the refresh backoff (60 s doubling to 1800 s for invalid_grant, else 300 s), parked with the terminal state; Go's background worker passes no failed token, so its terminal branch is the inline one. Only the first 401 per revision sets the time, narrower than Go's NextRefreshAfter.IsZero(): Go also keeps an existing reservation, and a metadata-only revision change restarts the rule here. The 96 h lead makes every Codex credential with 96 h or less left due at once on deploy, drained through the refresh workers without jitter; the no-expiry last_refresh fallback also moves from 24 h to 96 h. |

| M5-0001 | GET /management.html | covered | probe: GET /management.html -> 200; tests: crates/cpa-server/src/management.rs, crates/cpa-server/src/safe_mode.rs (+1) |  | The page is the embedded dashboard, not Go's downloaded panel (deliberate difference, see M5-0283). |

| M5-0304 | POST /v8/management/oauth/siwc/bind | partial | crates/cpa-server/tests/management.rs siwc_binding_flows_follow_go; crates/cpa-server/src/management/siwc.rs; crates/cpa-exec/src/codex_siwc.rs (authorize_url_matches_go, bind_file_keeps_disk_values_and_encodes_like_go, exchange_sends_the_issued_client_and_validates_the_grant) | manage | Start, callback, exchange, commit, cancellation, expiry, the legacy-callback refusal and the v8 list summary follow Go. The start reads its target under the management disk lock, as Go holds its commit gate (management::siwc::tests::a_bind_reads_its_target_only_under_the_disk_lock); the callback compares the percent-decoded path exactly; the commit refuses a file whose account changed since the start. The commit takes the credential's refresh lock, so an outer-token refresh in flight finishes first, even when its caller was cancelled (runtime::tests::a_cancelled_refresh_holds_the_preparation_lock_through_its_commit), and a cancelled binding keeps the lock until its commit returns (management::siwc::tests::a_cancelled_binding_holds_the_preparation_lock_through_its_commit), and both its rotated tokens and the binding are kept; Go reaches the same result by merging scoped patches. Not reproduced: Go's registration-epoch check (a target deleted and re-imported during the exchange is bound if its file, provider and account still match; M5-0247). After a binding is saved, an enabled target's unauthorized cooldowns are cleared, as Go's BindCodexSIWC does: model keys match a 401 or "unauthorized" in the error (Go's status-message check), the credential-wide key only Go's isUnauthorizedError. A quota block still live under a 401 stays blocked until the same time, as a quota cooldown with the quota's own status and reason (quota, cloudflare challenge or credential_quota), because Go keeps ModelState.Quota when recording a 401; the error becomes that reason only when it names "unauthorized", as Go rewrites only such a status message. The block is kept in memory beside the 401 and survives a restore while serving (save-cooldown-status turned on or moved), but not a restart, because saved records hold the 401 alone. Covered by the bound-step cooldown checks in siwc_binding_flows_follow_go and scheduler::tests clear_unauthorized_matches_go_per_key_kind, a_401_over_a_live_quota_keeps_the_quota_through_recovery and a_quota_under_a_401_keeps_its_kind_through_restore_and_recovery. Not covered: saving the cleared cooldowns (persist_cooldowns is called, but the integration test runs with cooldown saving off). Differences from Go: Go clears LastError, so its management output for the kept quota has no http_status, while Rust still reports the quota's status; Go also matches the error code "unauthorized", which cooldowns do not keep. In a live bounded window, a 401 whose body does not say "unauthorized" leaves the window as it is, which blocks the same as Go. Go's rejected access token has no Rust counterpart, because a 401 retry keys on the credential revision the binding replaces. Requests do not use the bound tokens yet: use_siwc in the Codex executor belongs to the executor work. |

| M5-0291 | internal/runtime/executor/codex_websockets_executor_store_test.go | covered | crates/cpa-server/src/websocket_tests.rs removing_a_credential_closes_only_its_upstream_sockets (TestManagerRemove_ClosesOnlyRemovedAuthWebsocketSessions); crates/cpa-exec/src/xai_ws_tests.rs a_removal_during_a_turn_keeps_the_replacement_sessions_ids |  | Removing a Codex or xAI credential from the store (CredentialStore reconcile, replace_config, replace_file_backed) ends only the sessions last dialed for it; a sibling connection keeps its socket for the next turn. An xAI session and its ID state leave together under the pool lock, and a turn takes the ID state after it holds its session, so a replacement session never keeps a dropped state. The other two cases hold by construction: cliproxy-rs builds its executors once per process and shares one session pool per provider, so no executor replacement or executor shutdown closes sessions (TestCodexWebsocketsExecutor_CloseAllReleasesSessions, TestManagerRegisterExecutor_PreservesSharedWebsocketSessionsAcrossExecutorReplacement). |

Paths match as in Go: one file (device and inode, or volume serial and file index on Windows), else Go's Abs and Clean spelling, case-insensitive and with verbatim prefixes written the ordinary way on Windows. Go's commit gate is the management disk lock, held from the invalidation through the write and publication. A mapped Windows drive whose folder is gone is not matched to its UNC spelling (ponytail in siwc.rs). |

| M6-0065 | Plugins discover/load native dynamic libraries, validate ABI and registration JSON schema,… | partial | crates/cpa-server/src/plugins.rs (sync, reload_plugin_auth_files); crates/cpa-plugin/src/host.rs, native.rs, store; crates/cpa-core/src/config/credentials/plugin_auth.rs; crates/cpa-server/src/runtime.rs persist_credential, write_unlocked (runtime::tests::a_plugin_virtual_refresh_never_rewrites_the_shared_source_file); tests/store_go.rs store_matches_go; cpa-server tests/plugin_routes.rs, tests/plugin_owned.rs plugin_parsed_auth_files_load_through_the_first_matching_auth_provider, a_refresh_committed_during_the_startup_reload_survives_it, a_reload_and_a_serial_plugins_save_both_complete, a_file_changed_after_its_parse_keeps_its_credentials_until_the_next_reload, the_startup_reload_lets_a_parse_use_the_runtime_with_one_worker; credentials/plugin_auth.rs a_locked_scan_only_uses_answers_cached_for_the_current_bytes; cpa-server watching::tests::a_deferred_file_keeps_a_refresh_committed_before_publication, a_new_auth_dir_publishes_a_plugin_bundle_only_once_parsed; management::auth_files::tests::an_upload_replaces_its_credential_while_plugins_parse_auth_files, a_refresh_between_an_upload_and_its_publication_survives; tests/plugin_owned.rs unloading_an_auth_provider_reloads_its_files_without_a_file_change, a_plugin_that_saves_its_file_while_parsing_ends_with_the_parse_of_the_saved_bytes, a_plugin_rewriting_its_file_on_every_parse_publishes_its_recent_parses, an_auth_identifier_recovering_from_an_error_reparses_the_auth_files, a_host_config_change_reparses_the_auth_files; credentials/plugin_auth.rs only_a_parses_own_write_publishes_its_answer_and_neither_is_cached | plugins | Loading, store installation, plugin catalogs and executors, translators and auth-file parsing are wired into the server, and plugin-virtual credentials are never written over their source file (results, refreshes and management edits). Plugins parse auth files holding no lock (preparse_auth_dir); the scan under the management disk lock takes their cached answers for the bytes it reads. A file changed since keeps the credentials the store holds under its reconciliation gate, and a new one is published only once parsed, until the watcher's next reload parses it (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md); an upload registers its built-in credential as Go's does. A change to what parses auth files (an auth provider loaded, unloaded, reconfigured, fused or identified differently, or the host config summary parse requests carry) makes the watcher reload the auth folder with no file change; a sync that changes none of them does not. When a file holds exactly the bytes a plugin's host.auth.save wrote to it during its parse, that parse's answer is published once and the watcher parses the saved bytes on its next tick, so the file ends with the parse of its latest bytes, as Go's watcher re-parses on the write's event (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md). Plugin auth refresh is wired (M6-0105); model.for_auth remains. Home plugin synchronization is tracked under M6-0239 (18/19 cases). |

Only refresh_interval schedules a background refresh (Go shouldRefresh without a lead). LastRefreshedAt and NextRefreshAfter are kept in memory per credential and registered with every change to the credential set, by Go's registration rule (prepareCoreAuthForModelRegistration): a credential registered again (an edit, a reload, a management change) keeps them while it and the one it replaces are both enabled, and otherwise (disabled or enabled again) takes the ones it is registered with, no LastRefreshedAt and the NextRefreshAfter its plugin parsed for it with that registration; a host.auth.save's auth takes those of the registered auth it replaces (Go findExistingAuthForSave and preserveExistingAuthState, unset ones included and under another ID too), else its own parsed one, once the manager registers it; a disabled saved credential ends with its own (the registration Go's watcher makes of the written file, DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md); a credential that leaves the set loses them (Manager.Remove), so one registered afresh, from the same file or another, starts from its own registration, and a refresh that started under an earlier registration of its ID is refused by the shared refresh writer, for every provider, before it merges or saves anything, and records nothing (Go's RegistrationEpoch: a registration number set when the ID enters the set and kept while it is registered again); a committed refresh's LastRefreshedAt is published with the credential, and a failed one reads the current credential; the startup load, made before plugins parse auth files, is provisional: the background refresh leaves it alone, and the first plugin-aware load registers each credential with its parsed NextRefreshAfter as Go's first load does, even when nothing else changed; a management status change registers without asking the plugin (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md); a failed or unsaved refresh from any trigger sets 5 min (capped at the current token's expiry, and not extended by the built-in refresh backoff), a refresh that leaves the credential due sets 30 s, and a refresh an unrelated edit made a no-op still records LastRefreshedAt. The saved document carries the response's disabled state and Go's merged proxy_url, which the running credential takes with it (a concurrent edit that wins the document wins both); a response that changes the provider or ID is refused, and prefix and attribute changes wait for the next load (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md). Built-in providers keep their own refresh, compat-named ones included (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md). Partial: with Home enabled Go refreshes these credentials through Home first (RefreshAuthViaHome); that is not ported, so Home mode leaves the compatible path off. |

| M6-0153 | RPC `host.auth.save` (constant `MethodHostAuthSave`); JSON envelope OK/result/error, byte … | covered | crates/cpa-plugin/src/hostauth.rs host_auth_save, build_auths_from_file_data, existing_auth_for_save; crates/cpa-plugin/src/auth.rs (Parsing, SaveParse); crates/cpa-server/src/management/plugin_auth.rs; crates/cpa-server/src/runtime.rs upsert_file; tests/plugin_host_auth.rs plugin_auth_callbacks_read_and_save_the_credential_store; crates/cpa-server/tests/plugin_owned.rs a_plugin_save_registers_what_the_auth_provider_parses, a_plugin_save_applies_the_files_settings_and_persists_as_go_does, a_nested_save_on_a_current_thread_runtime_skips_the_parse, a_save_from_another_thread_during_a_parse_of_its_file_is_checked_as_in_go, a_spawned_save_during_a_saves_parse_is_refused_and_the_recursion_stops, a_save_whose_parse_saves_the_same_file_completes_after_one_parse, an_auth_provider_can_save_while_it_parses, a_reload_and_a_serial_plugins_save_both_complete, a_plugin_that_saves_its_file_while_parsing_ends_with_the_parse_of_the_saved_bytes, a_plugin_rewriting_its_file_on_every_parse_publishes_its_recent_parses; management::plugin_auth::tests::a_plugin_save_waits_for_a_commit_holding_the_disk_lock |  | As in Go's buildAuthsFromFileData, the save asks the plugin auth provider for the file's type first, holding no lock: a failed parse fails the save before the write; each parsed auth's metadata is normalized before one auth inherits the file's other fields, and without its own ID it keeps the ID of the file's credential (as a built-in save does); only Go's save-time rules apply from the decoded file (priority, weight, prefix, proxy URL, disabled state and the auth's headers; the watcher applies the file's excluded models and aliases when it next parses it), and JSON null decodes to no fields as in Go; the parsed credentials replace the file's other ones (upsertAuthRecordsForPath). As in Manager.persist, only one auth that is neither runtime-only nor plugin-virtual and whose storage builds is persisted, and such an auth records its disabled state in its metadata first (FileTokenStore.Save); otherwise the file keeps the saved bytes and the auth is still registered. The save writes and registers under the management disk lock. Deliberate differences (DIFFERENCES-FROM-GO.md): a save from inside the same plugin's auth.parse of the file, on the parsing thread, writes without parsing again (Go recurses without bound); one from another thread while that plugin parses the file for a save is refused; a parsed auth naming another file is refused. |

The access provider comes from the authenticated Caller; nested host.model calls inherit the parent's through the callback scope. Gap: in Home mode Go's nested call shares the parent's mutable gin context, so a nested call whose own Home pick returns no key records the parent's Home key; Rust records the parent's original identity (needs a shared request-local Home identity). Plugin-executor usage records remain unported (M6-0174). |

| M6-0174 | internal/pluginhost/adapters_executors_usage_test.go | partial | 9/19 cases: crates/cpa-server/tests/plugin_owned.rs a_plugin_executor_publishes_the_usage_of_its_own_answer, a_plugin_executor_stream_publishes_its_usage_even_when_it_fails, a_plugin_executor_records_each_attempt_once_in_its_own_format (ExecutePublishesUsage, ExecuteThroughAuthManagerPublishesUsage, ExecuteAttributesResponsesUsageToSelectedAuth, ExecuteNilAuthSkipsUsage, ExecuteStreamPublishesUsage, ExecuteStreamThroughAuthManagerPublishesUsage, ExecuteStreamTwoCompleteChunksWithoutNewline, ExecuteStreamSplitAcrossBraceBoundary, ExecuteStreamErrorChunk) | plugins | Also tested: records carry executor type executorAdapter and the plugin provider, usage is read in the plugin output format, the reasoning effort comes from the translated payload, a dropped stream publishes one 499 record keeping its usage, a request that fails before the plugin is called publishes one executorAdapter failure, and count_tokens publishes nothing. By construction, not tested by name: ExecuteStreamNilAuthSkipsUsage (the model-router path passes no reporter), ErrorPublishesFailure (the attempt's failure path), ErrorChunkImmediatePublishWithoutClose (published as the error chunk is read), StreamSplitChunks and StreamLargeSSELineKeepsUsage (no line limit). Differs: a newline-free scalar remainder such as "data: null" is not flushed as Go flushes it (json.Valid accepts it), so the next chunk joins it and a usage line arriving that way is lost. Also differs: stream lines and their data: payloads are trimmed of ASCII white space only (trim_ascii in StreamUsage, extract_stream_json and the shared json_payload), where Go uses Unicode-aware bytes.TrimSpace, so a payload after data: led by \v or a no-break space is observed by Go and not by Rust. No counterpart: the two panic cases (plugin calls do not panic in process). Not ported: ObservePluginExecutorStreamTTFT_Antigravity (TTFT is the first chunk; per-protocol token detection is not ported, as for built-in executors) and the two UsageAdapter context-fallback cases. |

| M6-0267 | sdk/pluginhost/thinking_disable_type_test.go | covered | crates/cpa-plugin/src/api.rs ThinkingSupport.disable_type, thinking_support_carries_disable_type; crates/cpa-plugin/src/transform.rs plugin_model_info, plugin_model_info_keeps_the_disable_type; crates/cpa-server/src/plugins.rs model_spec, plugin_registered_sonnet_5_5_turns_thinking_off_with_between_tools |  | Both directions of Go's internal conversion carry it: a plugin model's DisableType reaches the registry (model_spec), and a plugin thinking applier receives the model's (plugin_model_info, Go registryModelInfoToPluginModelInfo). Go sdk/pluginhost registryModelToPluginModel serves the embedder SDK (Host.Models), which cliproxy-rs does not offer. |
